<p><i>Administrative Auxiliary Services (R1 121 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for personnel and administrative expenditure in respect of ministerial, parliamentary and other administrative staff at head office and for the purchase of supplies and equipment for the purpose of providing a general administrative and auxiliary service.</p>

<p><b>Programme 2: Mental Health (R2 286 000)</b></p>

<p>The amount of R2 286 000 required under this Programme covers expenditure under the various subprogrammes as follows:</p>

<block name="quote"><i>Subprogramme: Hospital Treatment (R562 000)</i></block>

<p>The amount required under this subprogramme will show a decrease of R71 000 due mainly to the fact that the expenditure on single-care grants calculated on the basis of the actual expenditure for 1987-88 will decrease.</p>

<p><span class="col_6771-6772" refersTo="page_0392"/>The following services are provided for under this subprogramme:</p>

<block name="quote"><i>Private Hospitals</i></block>

<p>The extended family system in the Indian community has inherent qualities of care, concern and responsibility. For this reason the majority of the mentally ill amongst Indians are cared for at home. A nominal provision of R1 000 is therefore made for the treatment of mentally ill patients at private hospitals.</p>

<p><i>Single Care Grants</i></p>

<p>A total of 210 patients are at present in the care of their parents or guardians. As from 1 October 1987 a grant of R162 per month, representing an increase of R20 over the previous financial year, is paid to each beneficiary.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Treatment of Outpatients (R1 723 000)</i></p>

<p>The amount of R1 723 000 required under this sub-programme will be used primarily for the rendering of community services to the mentally ill at departmental clinics and in the community. The treatment of patients on a community basis enables the patients to live with their families and enables them to take part in the open labour markets.</p>

<p>The personnel at present include two full-time specialist psychiatrists, one medical officer, six medical officers on a sessional basis, 30 nurses and six social workers. It is envisaged that a nursing services manager will be appointed during the financial year.</p>

<p><i>Departmental Clinics</i></p>

<p>There are at present 19 neuroclinics providing psychiatric services to the Indian community in operation throughout the Republic.</p>

<p>In addition to these clinics, it is proposed to establish further clinics at Marburg, Ladysmith, Umzinto and a further clinic at Unit 6 Chatsworth. An amount of R1 376 000 is provided for personnel and administrative expenditure as well as for the supply of medicines.</p>

<p><i>Community Psychiatric Services</i></p>

<p>An amount of R75 000 is provided for the payment of salaries of the staff who carry out home visit services in respect of patients who need intensive follow-up or patients who are not able to visit the clinics.</p>

<p>A further amount of R100 000 is provided for the payment of a subsidy to various homes and centres for the care of 34 profoundly handicapped children. This is 19 more than the last financial year.</p>

<p>Arrangements have now also been made with the Department of Education and Culture to use facilities available at the various special schools established by that department after school hours for vocational training of psychiatrically ill persons. An amount of R50 000 is required for the remuneration of part-time teachers/supervisors.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Certificates: Mentally Ill</i></p>

<p>A nominal provision of R1 000 is made for the certification of mentally ill patients by private practitioners.</p>

<p><b>Programme 3: Medical Care (R10 377 000)</b></p>

<p>The amount of R10 377 000 required under this Programme covers expenditure under the various subprogrammes as follows:</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: District Surgeon Services (R950 000)</i></p>

<p>The objective of this sub-programme is to provide curative services to indigent people, pre-employment examinations for Government employees and to perform medico-legal work. The department is responsible for District Surgeon services to the Indian community in the magisterial districts of Inanda, Chatsworth and Cape Town. The department now employs eight full-time medical officers and 10 medical officers on a sessional basis.</p>

<p>The major portion of the expenditure constitutes personnel expenditure.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Care of the Aged (R363 000)</i> <span class="col_6773-6774" refersTo="page_0393"/>Of the amount of R363 000, provision of R244 000 is for salaries in respect of personnel providing clinical services and health education to old aged persons.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Family Care R4 190 000)</i></p>

<p>The provision of R700 000 is made for the salaries of personnel providing clinical services and health education to families. Under this subprogramme provision is also made for R3 490 000 in respect of the Phoenix Community Health Centre, which was taken over from the Natal Provincial Administration by the Administration: House of Delegates on 1 September 1987. The R3 490 000 includes the following standard items:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Personnel:</p></td>

<td><p>R2 101 000</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Administrative expenses:</p></td>

<td><p>34 000</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Stores including medical supplies:</p></td>

<td><p>1 176 800</p></td>

</tr>

<td><p>18 000</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Professional and special services</p></td>

<td><p>160 500</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>It should be noted that this is an increase of R775 000 over the 1987-88 budget allocated to the Natal Provincial Administration. This will ensure that the Indian community will receive the highest standard of service and that the supply of medication, etc, will not be restricted as in the past.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Dental Services (R3 642 000)</i></p>

<p>An amount of R3 642 000 which represents an increase of R930 000 over the previous Budget is required to render dental services as follows:</p>

<p><i>Treatment</i></p>

<p>An amount of R973 000 is provided for the administration and running of five dental clinics in Natal and two in the Transvaal. Provision has also been made for new clinics to be established in Isipingo, Umzinto and Lenasia South. In the Cape Province one dentist has been appointed and seconded to the House of Representatives clinics in Rylands and Cravenby.</p>

<p>The amount of R2 311 000 is for the administration and running costs of the Oral and Dental Training Hospital of the University of Durban-Westville which, in association with the University, forms the Dental Training Centre for Indian dental therapists and oral-hygienists and a treatment centre for residents of its surrounding areas. The above amount also includes R112 000 for the personnel and administrative costs of the Dental Faculty in the process of being established.</p>

<p><i>Guidance, Prevention, Promotion and Training</i></p>

<p>An amount of R358 000 is provided primarily for salaries for personnel responsible for the organisation of the service so that effective dental health education programmes, prevention of dental diseases etc, which has been implemented, can be maintained.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: School Health Services</i></p>

<p><i>(R694 000)</i></p>

<p>An amount of R694 000 is required for personnel and administrative expenditure in respect of professional nursing staff who undertake the promotion of the health of Indian school children, so that they can attain the best possible level of physical and mental health, enabling them to achieve the maximum advantage from their school education. Children with defects are referred to the relevant authorities for assistance. Follow-up of health problems and education form an integral part of the service.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Administration (R538 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for meeting the costs of providing an administrative auxiliary service for the medical care programme.</p>

<p><b>Programme 4: Welfare Promotion (R156309000)</b></p>

<p><span class="col_6775-6776" refersTo="page_0394"/><i>Secretarial and Administrative Services (R4 718 000)</i></p>

<p>This subprogramme makes provision for the rendering of secretarial and administrative services to the Regional Welfare Board for Natal, and the newly established Regional Welfare Boards for the Transvaal and the Cape of Good Hope and welfare committees of Johannesburg, Cape Town and Port Elizabeth. It also makes provision for the overall administration of social welfare services.</p>

<p><i>Regional Welfare Boards and Committees (R144 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision has been made for the salaries of the secretarial assistants to the three above-mentioned boards and for the payment of expenses and allowances incurred by members of the boards and committees.</p>

<p><i>Welfare Administration (R4 574 000)</i></p>

<p>The amount of R4 574 000 provided for welfare administration includes salaries and expenditure of an administrative nature in regard to welfare services being provided to the Indian community, which includes, <i>inter alia-.</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(a) The administration of the Social Pensions Act, Act No 37 of 1973, which involves the processing of applications for social pensions.</li>

<li>(b) The subsidisation of old age homes in terms of the Aged Persons Act, Act No 81 of 1967.</li>

<li>(c) The administration of the Child Care Act, Act No 74 of 1983 (previously Children&#x2019;s Act, Act No 33 of 1960), which includes processing of applications for maintenance grants, the subsidisation of children&#x2019;s homes, the care of the children and registration of adoption of Indian children.</li>

</ol>

<p>In comparison to the 1987-88 financial year, an additional 21 administrative posts and three professional posts have been filled.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Care of the Aged</i></p>

<p><i>Old Age Pensions (R52 139 000)</i></p>

<p>The number of recipients as at 31 March 1988 was 27 032. As at 31 March 1987 there were 25 320 pensioners resulting in an increase of 1 762 pensioners. The amount required includes the additional amounts required as a result of the increase of R20 per month as from 1 October 1987. At present the maximum pension payable is R167 per month.</p>

<p><i>War Veterans&#x2019; Pensions (R696 000)</i></p>

<p>The total number of beneficiaries as at 31 March 1988 was 244. The qualifying age is 60 (male or female), but a war veteran who, due to his physical or mental disablement, is unable to tend to himself, may be awarded an attendance allowance irrespective of his age.</p>

<p>Proof of war service is essential and the same requirements in regard to citizenship and residence as under old age pensions are applicable. Parity with the amounts payable to Whites has been reached. The current pension payable as from 1 October 1987 is R233 per month.</p>

<p><i>Old Age Homes (R408 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for the payment of subsidies to old age homes. The following old age homes are being subsidised by my department: The Aryan Benevolent Home West Sun, Chatsworth, which has 57 residents, and the Aryan Benevolent Home, Pietermaritzburg, which has 18 residents.</p>

<p>As from 1 April 1988 the Red Cross old age home at Malabar, Port Elizabeth, will also be subsidised. <span class="col_6777-6778" refersTo="page_0395"/>It is expected that this will be in respect of 35 residents.</p>

<p>The subsidy is based on the average unit cost of all the homes for the aged, less 66,6&#x0025; of the average social pension or grant. On a similar basis 10 inmates are subsidised at two Coloureds old age homes, namely Gelvan Park Frail Aged Home in Port Elizabeth, and Huis Lodewyk Spies at Eersterus. It is anticipated that in the not too distant future, homes for the aged at Phoenix and Umzinto will be subsidised once applications have been received and finalised.</p>

<p><i>Service Centres (R200 000)</i></p>

<p>The department continues to subsidise the following service centres:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Clayton Gardens Home for the Aged</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014;29 inmates</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Durban Association for the Indian Aged, Phoenix</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014;43 inmates</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Durban Association for the Indian Aged, Chatsworth</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014;59 inmates</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Dayanand Gardens</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014;24 inmates</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>A subsidy calculated on 75&#x0025; of the average unit cost per aged person is payable up to a maximum of R447 per annum, calculated on the average daily attendance. A further non-recurring subsidy of 75&#x0025; of the actual purchase price of furniture and equipment is payable to a maximum of R200 per aged person per annum.</p>

<p><i>Subprogramme: Care of the Handicapped (R40 400 000)</i></p>

<p><i>Disability Pensions (R37 243 000)</i></p>

<p>The total number of beneficiaries for disability grants as at 31 March 1987 was 19 430. This figure increased to 20 029 as at 31 March 1988. At present the maximum pension being paid is R162 per month. The qualifying age for a disability pension is 16, male or female, and the pension is payable for as long as the beneficiary remains unfit for work or until he/she attains the qualifying age for an old age pension.</p>

<p><i>Blind Persons&#x2019; Pensions (R666 000)</i></p>

<p>The total number of beneficiaries as at 31 March 1987 was 299. As at 31 March 1988 the number had increased to 318. At present the maximum pension being paid is R167 per month. The qualifying age for a blind person&#x2019;s pension is 19, male or female, and the applicant must be registered as a blind person.</p>

<p><i>Lepers (R1 000)</i></p>

<p>A nominal provision is made.</p>

<p><i>Medical Costs&#x2014;Blind (R20 000)</i></p>

<p>A nominal provision is made.</p>

<p><i>Subsidies Blind/Deaf Students (R6 000)</i></p>

<p>A nominal provision is made.</p>

<p><i>Protective Workshops and Hostels (R1 104 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for the payment of subsidies up to a maximum of R88,58 per inmate per month in respect of the undermentioned institutions:</p>

<p>The new rate of R88,58 came into effect on 1 April 1987. The previous maximum was R83,83 per inmate. The number of inmates also increased from 270 to 316. In addition, a nonrecurring subsidy of 75&#x0025; of the total purchase price of furniture and equipment to a maximum of R350 per sub-economic disabled person per year is payable.</p>

<p><i>Homes for the Handicapped (R1 378 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for the payment of subsidies to the undermentioned homes for the handicapped:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Cheshire Homes</p></td>

<td><p>13</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Dayanand Gardens</p></td>

<td><p>258</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The subsidy is calculated on a unit cost based on the actual expenditure incurred but the subsidy is reduced by:</p>

<ol>

<li>(a) the amount of board and lodging&#x2014;662/3&#x0025; of the average social pension/grant and</li>

<li>(b) any other financial assistance from the State.</li>

</ol>

<p><span class="col_6779-6780" refersTo="page_0396"/><i>Subprogramme 4: Child Welfare</i></p>

<p><i>Departmental Places of Safety and Detention (R805 000)</i></p>

<p>An amount of R805 000 is provided for the administration and running costs of the Valley View and the Greenfields places of safety and detention. The latter institution came into operation on 1 December 1987 and can accommodate 80 children.</p>

<p><i>Special Grants-in Aid (R75 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for the payment of special grants to the management of children&#x2019;s homes for the purchase of necessary equipment, renovations etc provided such expenditure is incurred for the benefit of the children.</p>

<p><i>Children&#x2019;s Homes (R1 634 000)</i></p>

<p>Provision is made for the payment of subsidies to the undermentioned children&#x2019;s homes on the basis of the average unit cost of all subsidised children&#x2019;s homes:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Inmates</p></td>

<td><p>Aryan Benevolent Home</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Boy&#x2019;s Town&#x2014;Tongaat</p></td>

<td><p>55</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Lakehaven</p></td>

<td><p>80</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The unit cost differs from year to year and is determined when the homes&#x2019; financial statements are received.</p>

<p><i>Places of Care (R24 000)</i></p>

<p>Subject to approval from the Treasury, places of care/cr&#x00E9;ches can be subsidised on certain conditions. A nominal provision is made for the payment of such subsidies.</p>

<p><i>Maintenance Allowance (R43 920 000)</i></p>

<p>The total number of beneficiaries as at 31 March 1988 was 15 895 representing an increase of 888 over the figure of 14 871 as at 31 March 1987. The maximum parent&#x2019;s allowance being paid is R162 per parent per month with effect from 1 October 1987. The child&#x2019;s rate is R42 per child per month up to a maximum of four children per family with effect from 1 October 1987. In addition to this a special allowance of R7,50 per parent per month is payable if the beneficiary is a widow, widower, single, divorced or deserted person.</p>

<p><i>Foster Parent Allowance (R2 623 000)</i></p>

<p>A foster parent grant is payable to foster parents or guardians, irrespective of their income at the rate of R123.50 per child per month with effect from 1 October 1987.</p>

<p><i>Place of Safety Allowance (R350 000)</i></p>

<p>The allowance is paid to private institutions or places of safety for the care of children alleged to be in need of care, or children already found to be in need of care by the Children&#x2019;s Court, until such time as the placing of such children has been finalised. The present allowance is R4,06 per child per day.</p>

<p>The following statistics reflect the department&#x2019;s activities in the administration of the Child Care Act, No 74 of 1983, which replaced the Children&#x2019;s Act, No 33 of 1960:</p>

</tr>

</tr>

<td><p>323</p></td>

</tr>
